17a. 7 July 1727. Nagylibercse. Nógrád County reports that it prohibits the settlement of Jews within a radius of 7 miles of the mining towns, and that it has designated places for their settlement outside this area.

. . . that we should keep away and severely prohibit the Jews from attempting further access to our places adjoining the mining towns [submontanis], also in accordance with the dispositions of the same E. C. [Excelsum Consilium] already once graciously issued to us, and that we should specify those places which, in accordance with the aforementioned earlier disposition of the E. C., considering the distance of seven miles, have been permitted by us to them for free access; we humbly report that they have once again been severely prohibited through the renewed circulation of our determination established to that effect, and that, after the preliminary formalities, the places — namely the towns Gyarmath, Szécsény, Lossoncz and the villages adjoining them in this district, which are otherwise a good seven miles distant from the mining towns — will be permitted by us to them for free access.
From the general assembly held on 7 July 1727 in the estate of Nagy Libercse.

HU. Fasc. Mensium. Litt.


17b. 27 October 1727. Ludány. Nógrád County sends up its census of Jews.

Census of the Jews residing in the Illustrious County of Nógrád and the district of [OCR 'Kdöierlsi'; place name uncertain] . . . made and compiled:
Jacobus Sesaezni [OCR], residing in the estate of Gutta, in the house of the Lord General Antonius Jeszenszky, for two and a half years. Father of a family, has 3 sons: David, Benedict and Marcus, the first 13, the second 5 and the third 3 years of age. The said resident [hospes] Jacob formerly held and possessed from the above-named landlord, by title of lease, his allodial brewing house and distilling house [OCR 'crt-mutoriam'; reading uncertain], for one [year] and three quarters of a year, annually for florins [OCR 'no'; figure illegible], without, however, any use of external appurtenances. But since he is now oppressed by excessive misery owing to the dearth, and is burdened with debts arising from it, he resides in the same house simply and by the mere grace of the landlord, and has sustained [OCR 'ortam'; word uncertain] both himself and his children not otherwise than by his craft as a glazier (since the time of his residence in the bosom of this county, not contributing the least to the public or to domestic needs).
Aron Abraham, in the estate of Legind, in the house of Lord Suhajda, residing for about half a year, has sons Abraham, aged 2½ [OCR '2:{'], and Jacob, aged 1½; who, by the craft of a sock-maker or thread-maker [OCR 'filialificis'] and by a small trade in goods of spices worth about 4 florins, leads his life rather miserably. To his lord he pays annually fl. 4 and one pound of pepper, but to the public or for domestic needs nothing at all.
Loeephsie Samuel [OCR], in the estate of Szügy [OCR 'Sziigy'], in the house of Lord Stephanus Paczalay, residing for one quarter of a year, has sons Abraham, aged 5, Samuel, aged 3½, and Izrael, aged one; who, supporting himself and his children by mere trade in fringes and laces, sometimes also cloth, has paid his lord annually fl. 6, but to the public so far nothing.
Isak Jacob, in the same place, in the house of Lord Joannes Paczalay, likewise residing for a quarter of a year [OCR 'ab anno quadrante anni'; garbled], has sons Jacob, aged 10, Abraham

158

PDF viewer page 161

Source scan
…aged 5, Samuel aged 4 and Moyses aged 2. He lives with his children on a meagre trade, equal to that of the preceding person. He pays his lord fl. 6, but contributes nothing to the county treasury.
      Jacobus Vlcsek, residing in the same place, in the house of lord Stephanus Paczalay, for one year [OCR: "anno 1-0"]. As a lodger (inquilinus) he pays the same lord fl. 4 d. 50 annually, but pays nothing towards public or domestic needs. He has a son, Abraham, aged 4½. His trade is meagre, consisting of small quantities of spices and of cheaper girdles and laces. It should be noted here that this Hebrew is said [OCR garbled: "Haebrcus esse asseratur"] to have had fl. 1 exacted from him this year by the Jews of Szécsény by way of tax.
      Morcus Leuko, a lodger in the market town of Gyarmath, in the house of the most illustrious lady widow of Gabriel Balassa, has resided there for [OCR unclear: "a-bus"] years. He pays his lady landlord fl. 6 annually, but pays nothing towards public or domestic needs. He has a son, Laurentius, aged [OCR unclear: "a-ium"]. His trade, worth about fl. 60, consists of laces, girdles and at times also cheaper cloth. He too says that over the course of two years he has paid fl. 5 to the Jews of Szécsény, exacted by way of tax.
                            Josephus Péber, noble magistrate (judex nobilium) of the aforementioned illustrious county, in his own hand.

      In the year 1727, on the 10th day of the month of September, in the market town of Szécsén ... we, the undersigned noble magistrate and juror of the aforementioned illustrious county ... the mandate ordering ... the conscription of the Jews to be made ... We have carried out the conscription of the Jews residing in the district of Szécsény in the manner that follows, applying every diligence in investigating them.
      Elias Hnnszlo] [OCR unclear], aged about [OCR unclear: ":~D"], is a householder (hospes) residing in the market town of Szécsény. He has sons: Jacobus aged 17, a second, Samuel, aged 12, a third, Abraham, aged 10, and also Moyses, aged 7. Besides these, he has Salamon, a Jew, at present his servant, aged 17. He has a house there, built with a vault of unburnt bricks, in which he keeps, for the sake of trade, some smaller goods amounting to about 100 florins. He pays the most illustrious lord count, lord Joannes Adamus Forgacs de Ghymes etc., as landlord of the aforenoted market town, fl. Rhenish 12 by way of lease (arenda), without any [further] means. Together with the other Jews of the whole illustrious county, he customarily pays the renowned community (universitas) 12 Rhenish florins annually in the form of a tax.
      Morcus Laibl, aged about [OCR: "GO", possibly 60], is a householder residing in the same place. He has with him his son Samuel, aged about 24, who is married. He lives in his own house, which is small and built only of wattle, destitute of all means and lacking a vault. Together with his son he carries on a trade amounting to about 40 florins. He pays the above-titled lord count fl. Rh. 8 annually.
      llr ouses Laibl [OCR garbled; name uncertain], aged about 58, is a householder residing in the same place. He has a son Leo, aged 22, a second, Jacobus, aged 17, and a third, his son-in-law Isacus David, aged 30. The latter likewise has sons: Harsl, aged 16, Jacobus, aged [OCR unclear: ":1"], and Finus [OCR uncertain], aged half a year. He lives in a certain house abandoned by its own householder, with no means existing in it. He pays the above-titled lord 12 Rhenish florins annually and carries on a trade of about 30 florins, consisting of girdles, linen and smaller goods.

      Jacobus Leibl, aged about 43, is a householder residing in the same place. [He has] 5 little sons, of whom the eldest is Laib, aged 8; Hersl is aged 6 and Ezechiel aged 3; Saul and David, infants, are half a year old. He lives in his house, purchased there from a certain lodger for 7 florins. Though destitute of all means, he continues his residence there, and he pays the lord count 4 Rhenish florins annually.
      Isacus Marek, aged about 32, keeps himself there in the manner of a lodger for the sake of a modest trade and lodging. He nevertheless pays the most illustrious lord count 4 florins annually. He has no male offspring.
      Marcus Laibl junior, aged about 27, is a householder residing in the same place, without any means. He lives in a house there which he acquired himself. He has one son, Josephus, aged [OCR unclear: "B"], and another, Enoch, aged half a year. He likewise pays the oft-titled lord count 4 florins for lodging, and he has goods amounting to scarcely 10 Rhenish florins.
      Simon Samuel, aged about 40, is a lodger residing in the same place. He has two sons, Abraham aged 7 and Samuel aged one year. Formerly he served as a distiller here and there in the neighbouring villages. Now, however, because of his excessive guzzling of the same, he has been deprived of that service too. He pays the aforenamed lord count only 1 Rhenish florin annually, and he passes his life day by day more by precarious means than by trade, having entirely lost his credit.
      Jacobus Áron, aged about 35, is a lodger residing in the same place. Apart from one son aged 2, called Abraham, he has neither goods, nor a house, nor other means. Together with the aforenamed male child and several children of the female sex, he leads a miserable life solely through the goodwill and beneficence of the other Jews. He has no income and makes no payment on any title.
      Lev Benes, aged about [OCR unclear: "BO"], resides in the same place. Formerly, however, he leased a certain distillery house in the illustrious county of Heves, namely in the market town of Pászto. Last year a fire broke out there unexpectedly in the neighbourhood [OCR: "inaperante"], and he lost his leased house along with the rest, being left destitute of almost all household goods [OCR: "supolloot.ilc"]. Here he now keeps himself as a lodger, and therefore pays nothing either to the renowned community together with the other Jews or to the most illustrious lord count himself. At present, however, he carries about here and there on his back, for the sustenance of life, goods of about [OCR unclear: ":~O"] florins. He has one son, Benianus, aged [OCR unclear: "R"], and another, Moyses, aged 3.
      Salamon Moyses, aged about 70, is at present a lodger residing in the same place. He has with him his only son, called Jacobus, already married, aged about 25. This son is destitute of all kinds of merchandise. Solely by the permission and agreement of the Christian butchers there, he slaughters one beast or another on behalf of the other Jews and acts as a butcher according to their custom. He pays nothing either to the above-titled lord count or to the illustrious county.
      Marcus Hersl, aged about 40, acts there as teacher of the children of the other Jews of the illustrious county. He has a son, Jacobus, aged about [OCR unclear: "IH"]. He does not pay anything to anyone; rather, he himself receives his customary salary from the other Jews.
      Abraham Matis, residing on the estate (possessio) of Rimocz, aged about 25. He has one brother, Samuel, aged 17, and another, Jacobus, aged 12, all unmarried. He lives only in a kitchen that was once built for a captain quartered there. To the aforesaid estate, both for lodging and also in

      160

PDF viewer page 163

Source scan
respect of the lease of the very meagre revenue of the butcher's shop he operates there, he pays the aforesaid estate [OCR unclear: "ti"] Rhenish florins annually. He customarily helps proportionately in the payment of the 12 florins usually rendered to the renowned county.
      Abraham, aged about 41, is a householder residing on the estate of SúrDg [OCR unclear]. He has two sons. One of them, Abraham, aged about 13, is at present in service with a certain Jew of Brody named Jacobus Aller, who otherwise often frequents these parts with merchandise. The other is Moyses, aged [OCR unclear: "H"]. He lives in a lodger's house of the most illustrious and noble lord Adamus Roth, which has no appurtenances. He pays the landlord fl. 24 annually and contributed some groats towards the payment of the aforementioned 12 florins. He has goods amounting to about 20 Rhenish florins.
      Baruch Abraham, aged about 38, is a householder residing on the estate of Bagyon. He has one son called Abraham, aged about 10. With him lives his kinsman Todres David, married, aged 25. They live in a house of the noble lord Georgius Balogh de Ocsa in the manner of lodgers, without appurtenances, carrying on trade by carrying smaller goods on their backs. They contribute 6 Rhenish florins annually to the same lord, and help proportionately with some amounts towards the payment of the 12 florins usually rendered to the illustrious county.
      Jacobus Bernard, aged about 40, is a householder residing on the estate of Bujak. He has with him his son-in-law Marcus Abraham, aged about 28, and also a son called Cain, aged half a year. In addition there is a sister's son, Isacus Laibl, at that time in service with him, aged about 22. He lives in the manner of a lodger in a house [OCR: "Domogys."] without a vault and without any appurtenances, belonging to the most illustrious lord count, lord Stephanus Eszterházy de Galaritha [OCR; sic] etc. For its lease he pays the above-titled lord count 6 Rhenish florins annually, and towards the payment of the 12 florins he pays some Rhenish florins together with the other Jews.
      Done in the year, day, month and place as above.
        Adamus Bene de Nándor, noble magistrate of the illustrious county of Neograd, in his own hand.
        Ladislaus Bohus, juror of the same illustrious county of Neograd.
        Read by Stephanus Gyürky, ordinary sworn notary of the illustrious county of Neograd, in his own hand.
       O. L. C 29. No 1. 1727-10.   Canc. Acta Partic. No 269-16.



         18 a. 1727, April 7. Galgóc. Nyitra County reports that those Jews who live closer than 7 miles to the mining towns must be resettled farther away, and that it has also notified the landlords of this.

       "... to publish within this entire County of ours. Thereafter, should any Jews perhaps still be found lurking within the circuit of 7 miles of such Royal Mining Towns, they are to send them away without delay. Moreover, they are also to admonish the Landlords, under the penalty for non-observance of the gracious Royal Mandates, not to dare to give any further place or protection to such Jews, who are to be removed from such a circuit of 7 miles of the Mining Towns. Should any perhaps be found more recalcitrant, they shall not fail to report such persons [OCR unclear: "inaudita"]."
       O. L. Htt.   Litt.

 11 Oklevéltár   VII.

19.      1727, September 15. Pest. Pest County submits the census of the Jews.*

                                          Market town of Sámbék.
Jacub Isák has a wife and
      sons Eliam and Isak ............................................  No. 2.
      No kinsman with him,
      Jewish servant Jakub .............................................  No. 1.

           without appurtenances.
      Came here this year.
      To the treasury [gremium] of the County he at present pays nothing,
      but to the Domain in common with others, as will be specified below.

The present [OCR: 'Praesenpti'] Jews of Sámbék all came to the aforementioned town this year; to the treasury of the County or of the Community they have so far paid nothing, but to the Domain, by agreement, namely with the Most Excellent Lady Countess Petra [sic in OCR] Zichy [Zichiana], they pay annually in total fl. 120. Besides these there are, separately, those who pay to the Domain, residing in the said town of Sámbék, namely:
